# In-House ERP: Micro-Checklist & Build Tracker

The build tracker, arranged by ERP module: **24 modules**, each split into sub-modules that hold the items. The skeleton is permanent - a module or sub-module with nothing left still shows, marked *Nothing open.* Built items move to **[the archive](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md)**, which has the same skeleton, so a module's history sits in the same place in both files.

- **Items.** `[ ]` open, `[x]` built and verified (build/vet/test, plus a live check where the item calls for it). Each item ends with `*Status: ...*` - Not started · Building · Built, not verified · Done · Needs decision · Blocked - set when the item is recorded and updated once more when the work finishes or blocks.
- **Ids.** An item keeps its id for life; code comments cite them (e.g. `47.11.4`). The number before the first dot is the ask the item came from - these were called stages until 2026-11-10. The write-ups of the old stage sections are in the archive's **[Background notes](archive/micro_checklist_closed_stages.md#background-notes)**, filed by module; an ask since then has its run header in the **Asks log**.
- **Every ask is an item (user rule, 2026-10-18).** Whatever the user asks - a large piece of work or a one-line fix - is recorded before building starts: a run header at the end of the **Asks log** (date, the user's words, the item ids, a status) and each item in the sub-module it changes. A new ask takes the next free number - `pwsh docs/update-checklist-map.ps1` prints it - and numbers its items N.1, N.2, ...; never open a `## Stage` section. Nothing the user said may exist only in chat. New asks add no Background notes: context goes in the run header, the narrative once in `project_ledger.md`.
- **Where an item goes.** The sub-module a user would look under for that screen or behaviour: a PO screen fix is *PROC › Purchase orders*, not *UI*; *UI* holds only what every screen shares (menus, window fit, inputs, wording). One item, one place. A new sub-module only when nothing fits, added to both files in the same position; a new module only on the user's say-so.
- **Counts.** After editing either file run `pwsh docs/update-checklist-map.ps1`: it refreshes the Module map below and the *Nothing open.* markers; `-Check` reports drift without writing.
- **Archiving.** An archive pass moves `[x]` items, with their sub-items, to the same sub-module in the archive. An item with an open sub-item, an open decision or an unverified status stays here.

> **Legend for historical notes below**: some items were marked `[x]` early and later found to be missing real implementation — those carry a "re-opened"/"correction" note explaining the gap and the fix. This is expected project history, not something to clean up.

Running it on a client is the owner's step. *Status: Built, not verified on a real drive*

### PIM › Pricing, MRP & market prices

- [ ] **34.4** JSON-endpoint harvester, **stdlib only**. This is feasible precisely because of the reference doc's headline finding: **Myntra ships its whole search result set as an embedded `window.__myx` JSON blob** (fallback `window.__m_initial_state`), so `net/http` + `regexp` + `encoding/json` are sufficient — **no browser and no HTML parser**. Adopt the retired project's two-phase shape: harvest raw responses to disk under a bounded worker pool, skip any file already present and non-trivial (free resumability *and* cache), then parse offline in a second pass so a parser bug costs no re-fetch and no re-ban. **[needs user decision — gated on 34.6]** Ask 168 (erp-0b, 2026-11-13): built for Myntra only - `platform.market_harvest_myntra` (off), max pages and interval settings; robots.txt honoured, no redirects followed, generic User-Agent, data per client; PIM > Reports > Market prices runs it (admin). PIM-9340..9343. Tests on saved pages. Not run against myntra.com: needs the owner's go-ahead. *Status: Built, not verified live*

- [ ] **149.2 PIM ▸ Workbench does not load.** User, 2026-10-31: *"PIM workbench is not loading look into it"*. Cause: `ListWorkbench` scored each Item one by one (~10 queries plus a profile write per Item, up to 2000 Items) - 63 Items took 1.3-2.0 s locally, so a full catalog runs past the 55 s request deadline. Fix: five set-based queries share `scoreCoreCompleteness` / `deriveProfileStatus` with `CalculateCompleteness`; changed profiles go back in one batched upsert (`last_scored_at` now moves only on a change). Verified locally: all 63 Items score as the single-item path does; new `TestPIMWorkbenchBulkMatchesSingleItemScore` (family attributes, blank locale override, pending content, Published kept); 2000 Items 2.3 s cold (every profile written) then ~0.25 s. Shipped in 0.21.0; needs a look at PIM ▸ Workbench on app / minn. Also (not yet committed): the Family filter box drops `.table-panel`'s 200 px min-height. *Status: Built, not verified*

### PROC › Goods receipt

- [ ] **173.1 A step receipt's bill rounding leaves inventory valuation a few paise off** [needs design decision: which account takes the paise]. Found by 74.U.2's E8 run (erp-2b, 2026-11-14): a PO at 200 GST-inclusive, 5 pieces received in steps at 190.48 and posted, puts stock in at 952.40. The bill, at 1000 inclusive, clears AP at 952.38 and, under 148.51, moves the 0.02 to Inventory (1200). Stock stays at 952.40, so `inventory-valuation` and `cat-stock-valuation-summary` are red by 0.02 per such line. **Recommended:** post the bill's stock rounding to Round Off (5280, as a bill's round-off line already does). AP still clears to the paisa, 1200 equals stock value, and books already posted do not move. The other answer, writing the paise into the item's stock value, touches valuation layers for no business gain. *Status: Needs decision*

## INV — Inventory & Planning

### INV › Stores & stock locations

- [ ] **106.5 From / To stock location at the top of every transaction** (user, 2026-10-19: "I want a location selection in TOP in all txn like store. It will be frozen where not changable. Else Drop down. Need From & TO in all txn ... Freze there but Changable should be dropdwon. Ex- In TO/POS, If I alliow multiple location drop down will allow to change."). Every transaction screen shows From and To beside the store selector, read from Stock Locations > Transactions (106.4): fixed text where the row is locked, a drop-down where it is editable, and the document then moves stock from / to what was picked. Depends on 106.4. Decision (user, same day, recommended): the drop-down changes that one document only (starts at the setting); it is a drop-down only when the row is editable and the store uses more than one fitting location, else fixed text; POS, Goods Receipt, Transfer Out / In, Purchase Return, Sales Return, Stock Move, Damage. *Built 2026-10-22:* `txnStockFromTo` / `mountStockFromTo` / `readStockFromTo` (txn-screen.js) read `GET /api/v1/stock-locations/txn-header` (`StockTxnHeaderFor`): a drop-down where the row is editable and the store has more than one fitting location (POS: sellable only), else fixed text. On GRN (follows Receiving Location), Transfer (follows From Warehouse) and its receive dialog, Purchase Return (the GRN's store), POS (view-pos.js +53 B gz) and a return's Inspect. The document's own `stock_from` / `stock_to` overrides the setting for that document only (`drawFromDefaultFromTx` / `applyReceiptDefaultTx` take it; locked sides ignore it); a return's QC stamps it (`SetDocumentStockField`). Stock Move / Damage already ask on their form. Verified: `TestStage1065DocumentStockFromTo` + 1064; Chrome strips on GRN / Transfer / POS; a TO saved with `stock_from` keeps it. USER_GUIDE Stock Locations paragraph. Ask 168 (erp-0b, 2026-11-13): checked in Chrome: the strip is on GRN, Transfer, Purchase Return and POS; a transfer received to a picked To landed there. A GRN posted with a picked To and the POS sale are only test-covered (`TestStage1065DocumentStockFromTo`). *Status: Built, not verified - those two not walked*

### POS › Store app (phone / tablet)

- [ ] **47.6 Real mobile/RF WMS/POS shell and physical reference journey** — closes A-06/A-39; not a desktop page squeezed into 390 px. **47.6.1-47.6.5 and 47.6.7 are built and green 2026-09-06.** The parent stays `[ ]` on **47.6.6 alone**, which needs physical hardware (device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption run) and cannot be done from a development machine.
  - [x] **47.6.1** `engines/field_semantics.go` — an explicit semantic registry (`text`, `phone`, `barcode`, `wave`, `lpn`, `lot`, `serial`, `number`, `date`) that **wins over** the substring inference, served to the browser by the existing `/api/v1/meta/field-formats` endpoint so the two sides cannot reach opposite answers. The A-06 bug reproduced and fixed: `mobile-pick-wave-id` contains "mobile", matched the phone tokens, and the phone keystroke filter stripped the letters out of a wave id **as the operator typed it**, silently, on a device where nobody could see the rewrite. **The more dangerous half was server-side** and is also fixed: `IsPhoneField` feeds `NormalizeDocumentPhones`, so without the override the server would have rewritten a stored wave id or lot number into E.164 on every save — mangling data at rest, not just while typing. The inference is deliberately **not** removed wholesale: its own comment explains it covers every phone field in the schema today and every one added later for free, and removing it would have silently stopped normalising all of them. `engines/field_semantics_test.go` asserts both directions — scan fields lose their filter, real phone fields keep theirs, `phone_country` stays excluded.
  - [x] **47.6.2** The RF task shell (`renderRFTraceabilityView`, `public/app.js` + `.rf-*` in `styles.css`): no sidebar or top bar while a task is open (`body.rf-shell`, cleared in `renderViewContent` — the one place every view change passes through, so a bad exit cannot leave the app permanently chromeless), one instruction in words at the top, one scan target that keeps focus and re-takes it after every action, Enter advancing to the next field rather than submitting a half-filled form (a hardware scanner sends Enter after every barcode), a human label beside every code, no naked UUID, and 44px+ targets throughout. The supervisor path is a new **`FloorAssistRequest`** doctype the operator raises from their own session and a supervisor answers on their own device — explicitly **not** a Grievance (that is an HR complaint record; filing "I cannot scan this bin" into somebody's HR file is both wrong and quietly harmful) and explicitly **not** a password handoff, which is how shared logins start on a floor.
  - [x] **47.6.3** Built as **one responsive layer**, not six screen patches — every one of the six screens is assembled from the same vocabulary (`.table-panel`, `<table>`, `.form-input`, `.btn`), so fixing the vocabulary fixes all six *and* every screen added later; patching six screens would have fixed six screens. Three changes, all inside `@media (max-width: 820px)` so the desktop rendering is byte-identical: the sidebar goes **off-canvas** as a drawer (it was a hard 270px with no collapsed state anywhere in the stylesheet), `.table-panel` becomes a horizontal scroll container, and filter/action rows wrap. Plus a 400px reflow block for WCAG SC 1.4.10.
    **The measurement that matters, and a correction to how it was first taken:** `documentElement.scrollWidth` reported zero overflow *even before the fix*, because `.app-container` and `.main-content` are both `overflow: hidden` — so this application never scrolls sideways, it **clips**, which is worse and is exactly what "no *hidden* horizontal overflow" means. Re-measured against the right instrument (elements whose right edge passes a clipping ancestor that cannot be scrolled to reveal them), at 390×844 in Chromium:
    | | content width | sidebar cost | worst unreachable clip |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | before | 120px of 390 | 270px | POS 475px, Returns 553px — **7 of 7 screens** |
    | after | 390px | 0px | **0px on all 7** |
    Verified at 390 portrait, 844 landscape and the 320px reflow threshold, across POS, RF receiving, putaway, wave picking, sortation, loading dock, Returns and the RF shell, with zero console errors. The drawer was checked for keyboard operation too (toggle visible, `aria-expanded` tracking, scrim, Escape closes, focus returns).
  - [x] **47.6.4** The six-outcome vocabulary 47.6.4 names is complete and lives in one table (`RF_OUTCOMES`): success, duplicate, **wrong item**, **owner mismatch**, **hold**, offline — six because an operator must tell them apart *without reading*, and "already scanned" versus "the thing in your hand is not the thing on the screen" call for opposite next actions. Each carries a distinct tone, vibration rhythm, colour **and** border weight/style **and** text — four channels, because an aisle is loud (sound alone fails), bright (colour alone fails) and an operator may be colour-blind (colour alone fails again). Outcomes are resolved from the message-catalog **code**, not the HTTP status, since INVENT-0104/0106/0115 are all 422 and mean three different things on the floor — the first caller to actually use the precise coding `engines/traceability.go` has always returned.
    WCAG 2.2 AA work: a **global** `:focus-visible` ring (there were rings on ~8 components out of the whole application, so keyboard/scanner navigation was invisible everywhere else — SC 2.4.7), raised above sticky headers (SC 2.4.11, new in 2.2), and a 24×24 minimum target size on `.action-btn`/`.btn`/`.icon-btn` (SC 2.5.8, new in 2.2 — the AA threshold is 24×24, not 44×44, which is AAA).
    **Four real AA failures were found and fixed, by measurement rather than by eye:** `--warning-strong` on `--warning-soft-bg` was **2.86:1** in the light theme (fixed to 6.37:1); `--on-primary` on `--primary-color` was **3.90:1** in *both* dark blocks (fixed with the standard on-colour flip to dark-on-accent, 4.58:1, rather than darkening the accent — which would have cost it its contrast against `--panel-bg` and made primary buttons recede); six form controls had adjacent `<label>`s with **no `for=`**, so they were visible but silent to a screen reader; and two controls (`global-search`, `industry-selector`) had no accessible name at all.
    All of it is guarded by `internal/server/frontend_accessibility_test.go`, which parses `styles.css`/`index.html` and fails the build on regression — the same self-checking shape `route_capabilities_test.go`, `scope_policy_test.go` and the Stage 49 manifest test already use, because a one-time accessibility sweep lasts until the next screen. It computes real WCAG contrast ratios across **all three** token blocks (light, explicit dark, and the `prefers-color-scheme` default — a token correct in one and wrong in another is wrong for every user who never touched the theme toggle, which is most of them).
    **What this does NOT claim:** full AA conformance across all ~40 dynamically-rendered screens. That needs axe-core driving a browser over each one; what is guarded here is the foundation (tokens, focus, target size, reflow structure, shell labelling) plus the six screens measured above. The test says so in its own header rather than letting a green run imply certification.
  - [ ] **47.6.6** Device/scanner/printer certification and the gloves/weak-Wi-Fi/interruption/app-restart run. **[physical hardware/user input required — cannot be done from here]**
  - [x] **47.6.7** All four orphaned endpoints now have a caller, built into the task shell rather than as a sixth desktop WMS screen exactly as this item asked: `POST /api/v1/wms/batch/putaway` (bin-level lot assignment — what makes a lot visible to FEFO at all), `/batch/consume`, `/batch/expiry-sweep`, `/serial/status`. Live-verified against a scratch server on :8099: all four reachable and validating (422 on an empty body, 200 for the no-argument sweep), and the semantic registry confirmed served with `mobile-pick-wave-id → wave`.

  - **Four unrelated defects were found and fixed while doing this**, all recorded here so nobody re-derives them: (1) the RF shell CSS from 47.6.2 hid `.top-bar`, a class that does not exist in this markup - the header is `.app-header` - so the shell was still rendering the desktop search bar and industry selector above the task; (2) `db/migrations_stage47_2_*.sql` was not self-correcting on `POSPriceOverride.location` - an early draft declared it optional and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` will not update an existing row, so any database that applied that draft would have read as unscoped for ever and leaked one store's override evidence to every other store; a follow-up `UPDATE` now makes the correction part of the migration. (3) `cmd/surfacescan`/`internal/securityscan` walked into `.claude/worktrees/agent-*` - full, **gitignored** copies of this repository that concurrent agent sessions leave on disk - and counted every `engines/*.go` outbound call site once per worktree, so `TestAttackSurfaceManifestIsCurrent` and `TestNoUnreviewedBypassPattern` failed for everyone the moment any session had a worktree open, for reasons unrelated to the code under review; `.claude` added to both scanners’ skip lists (`skippedDirs` in `bypass.go` already skipped `.git`/`node_modules`/`graphify-out`/`docs`, and `surface.go` repeated the same list inline, so both needed it). (4) `cmd/brainmap` emitted `BRAIN.md`'s lobe diagram in Go's randomised map order (sorted on weight alone, and `sort.Slice` is not stable), so `docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` failed at random on its own `-check` step - the redraw this repo's conventions require after any change that adds or moves a file. Fixed with the same (weight, from, to) tiebreak the region-edge sort in that file already used; verified deterministic over three paired runs.
- [ ] **102.1.2 Camera scanning** - the phone camera reads barcodes where the browser can (BarcodeDetector); a Bluetooth scanner works as a keyboard everywhere; typing the code always works. The camera is allowed for `/store/` only (Permissions-Policy), the rest of the ERP keeps it off. **Built 2026-10-19:** `permissionsPolicyFor` (`internal/server/middleware.go`) gives `/store` and `/store/*` `camera=(self)`, every other path `camera=()` (test `TestPermissionsPolicyCameraOnlyOnStoreApp`; checked live on both paths); full-screen camera with a frame, rear camera, Code 128 / EAN / UPC / Code 39 / ITF / QR / Data Matrix where supported, vibrate on a read, closes when the app is hidden. A scanner without Enter is caught as a burst of fast keystrokes (Stage 77's rule). **Not verified:** desktop Chrome on Windows has no BarcodeDetector, so the walk saw the "type or use a Bluetooth scanner" note instead of the camera - the camera path needs a real Android phone (102.5.2). **Limitation:** iPhone Safari has no BarcodeDetector either; reading barcodes there would need a JavaScript decoder library, ruled out by the no-dependency rule - iPhone users type or use a Bluetooth scanner (said in both guides). *Status: Built, not verified*
- [ ] **102.1.3 Push alerts** (Web Push) for approvals and low stock. **Built 2026-10-22:** `engines/web_push.go` - RFC 8291 aes128gcm + RFC 8292 VAPID with the stdlib (byte-for-byte RFC 8291 vector test); a key pair per client, private half under the new `WEB_PUSH_KEY` keyring; subscriptions per phone (`web_push_subscriptions`), sent only to the browsers' push services; a worker each minute diffs each subscriber's task-inbox approvals (every path into Pending Approval is covered) and the store's low stock (the Inventory Dashboard's rule, now one shared `inventoryLowStock`), pushes each thing once (`web_push_seen`; the first look only takes note), through the job runner (`web_push_delivery`; 404/410 end the subscription; nothing sent with side effects off or for a sandbox). App: Settings > Alerts; `sw.js` push + notification click. Verified: engine tests incl. delivery to a local push service that checks the VAPID signature and decrypts the message, and the worker (one push per new approval / low item, none on a repeat). Headless Chrome has no notification permission, so subscribing and showing an alert are the user's check (102.5.2). Found and fixed on the way: the Inventory Dashboard's low-stock list read Postgres integers as 0 and listed in-stock items as low. *Status: Built, not verified on a phone*
- [ ] **102.5.2 Real phones** - Android Chrome and iPhone Safari: install, scan, offline count, push alert; anything only the user can check on store hardware recorded as such. The user's checks: Android Chrome - install, camera scan (102.1.2), Settings > Turn on alerts, then an approval sent to you and an item sold out each show an alert that opens the right screen (102.1.3), a count in flight mode sent when back online; iPhone Safari - Add to Home Screen, alerts (iOS 16.4+), typing / Bluetooth scanner; a store PC with PrintBridge printing a Store Label Request from Print from Transaction (102.3.4); a basket number typed or scanned into the till's Find a basket (102.4.2). *Status: Needs the user's phones*

### RPT › Report engine, filters & exports

- [ ] **26.10.6** [P2 — tier/scope decision] Dedicated data mart/read replica for heavy BI queries — only justified once real report-query load is measured against the live Postgres instance; don't build ahead of a measured bottleneck, per `CLAUDE.md`'s lightweight-first principle. **Go-ahead given 2026-07-27 covered all 5 P2 bundles, but this one item is deliberately NOT built as a result**: its own gating condition is "measure first," and there is still zero measured query-load evidence to build against — building a data mart anyway would be exactly the speculative-build this item was written to prevent, not a case of following a decision that's now been made. Built 26.10.7 instead (the measurement mechanism itself), which is what actually lets this item's own criterion ever be evaluated.
  - **Measured 2026-08-06, and the answer is still "not justified" — now on evidence rather than absence of evidence.** Re-greenlit by the user this date; measured before building, per the item's own condition. The live production database holds **no transactional data at all**: largest tenant table is `doctype_fields` (722 rows, metadata), `documents` 210, `audit_logs` 251, and `gl_postings`/`POSCart`/`SalesInvoice` are **empty**. Four user accounts. There is no query load, therefore no bottleneck, therefore nothing a read replica would relieve — it would add a replication topology, a second connection path and a staleness window to a system whose reports currently return instantly. Reported back to the user rather than building anyway.
  - **Concrete revisit thresholds recorded** (check via Reports → Report Performance, the 26.10.7 screen): any report's p95 duration over ~5s, **or** total report time over ~10% of DB busy time, **or** report queries measurably slowing transactional work (watch `db_pool.wait_duration_ms` on `/api/v1/health`). Written into `go_live_decisions.md` §12 so the condition is checkable by anyone, not just re-litigated.

The signing chain itself was already proven (unchanged, checked by eye/tests).
- [ ] **31.1.9 Replace QZ Tray with our own lightweight agent (PrintBridge) — Windows deployable; macOS built but never run on a Mac; physical-printer + live-ERP print open.** Opened from *"Can we build our own QZ tray?"*. Licensing fear doesn't apply (QZ is LGPL-2.1); no surveyed alternative adoptable; a rebranded `qzind/tray` build worked but was scrapped at 138.5MB. **Built:** clean-room **Go** agent at `../PrintBridge/` speaking QZ's wire protocol exactly, so `public/qz-print.js` needs **zero changes**; zero dependencies, no LGPL obligation; covers 100% of what the ERP calls. **Deployment = one URL:** double-click `PrintBridge\Make-Installer.cmd`, type the client's ERP address → it produces **both** `printbridge-setup-<host>.exe` (Windows, 2.8MB) and `printbridge-mac-<host>.zip` (macOS, 6.5MB). No certificate files: the agent trusts the browser-reported `Origin` against the configured address (exact scheme+host+port), signatures still verified. **Verified on Windows:** origin-trust attack tests; real Chromium trusts the configured origin and refuses any other with the same page+cert; 10/10 protocol tests over `ws://` and `wss://`; raw ZPL byte-exact to a real queue; PDF via WinRT; HTML via headless Edge; install→upgrade→uninstall clean; logo embedded in exe/installer/tray via a hand-written resource object; the installed agent's log shows the **real ERP client** (cert "Custom ERP Print Authority") completing the handshake, correctly refused from unconfigured dev ports 8168/8169. **macOS (2026-10-18, client has a Mac):** CUPS backend (`lp`/`lpstat`, LANG=C), LaunchAgent start-at-login, keychain trust via `security` for https ERPs, double-click `PrintBridge.app` installs / shows status / uninstalls through native `osascript` dialogs; universal Intel+Apple-Silicon binary and the .app zip are assembled **on Windows** by `agent/cmd/mkmacapp`. Verified without a Mac: darwin/arm64, darwin/amd64 and linux build + vet clean; CUPS-output parsing, LaunchAgent plist, AppleScript escaping and .icns unit-tested; zip re-read confirms the exec bit, a valid universal Mach-O, and the ad-hoc code signature M-series Macs require; minimum macOS read from the binary (13.0). **No menu-bar icon on Mac** (needs AppKit/cgo). **Unsigned:** each Mac needs a one-time System Settings → Privacy & Security → *Open Anyway*; removing it needs Apple notarisation ($99/yr Apple Developer account) [needs decision: user]. **Also fixed:** a second copy started from the Start menu while running (now single-instance); `$PSScriptRoot` empty in a PS 5.1 param default broke `Make-Installer` on double-click; Roaming-dir TLS private key; empty GUI-build log; leaked trust roots; silent-install certificate prompt. **Open:** (1) **first real Mac install** — expect it to surface something; (2) physical print on the attached `Y468BT Label` (same gap as 31.1.8); (3) a real print driven by the running ERP; (4) Linux has no installer; (5) USB/HID/serial/file/status-listener calls unbuilt (ERP uses none). Marketplace-panel printing is a separate workstream.

- [ ] **90.1 The rule and the stamp.** Semantic versioning from the commits since the live build: any `feat:` → MINOR (0.1.0 → 0.2.0); only fixes / docs / chores → PATCH (0.2.0 → 0.2.1); MAJOR only by hand (`deploy.ps1 -Version 1.0.0`). `engines/release_versions.go` `NextReleaseVersion`; `cmd/releasever` reads `git log <live commit>..HEAD`. `deploy.ps1` reads the live version + commit from the box's `/api/v1/version`, gets the number, and stamps `-X internal/server.releaseVersion`. `currentAppVersion()` prefers the stamp, else the VERSION file, so the sidebar, `/api/v1/version` and the Control Panel all follow. Tests: `TestNextReleaseVersion`, `TestStampedReleaseVersionWins`; `cmd/releasever` gives 0.1.1 from 840909b (docs only) and 0.2.0 from 0f2e6e1 (a feat). *Status: Built, not verified live*

## SEC — Identity, Access & Security

### SEC › Sign-in, sessions & MFA

- [ ] **SB-021** `tenant_default.cashier1` and `tenant_default.system` still carry the shipped bootstrap password hash from `db/migration.sql`. **`system` is an internal FK-anchor account that never logs in interactively — safe to set `Inactive`.** **`cashier1` is a real POS login a real cashier uses day to day** — rotating its password is a product/operational decision (a new password has to be chosen and told to the actual store), not something to guess at unilaterally during a deploy. **[needs user decision]**
- [ ] **49.2 Identity, authentication, recovery and machine identity** — prevent account takeover without making safe recovery impossible. — *2026-09-08: 49.2.2 closed. 2026-09-09: 49.2.4 closed (see its own note). Parent stays open pending 49.2.1, 49.2.3, 49.2.5-49.2.8.*
  - [x] **49.2.1 Identity lifecycle.** Unique human identity; no shared cashier/admin/support account; joiner/mover/leaver approval; immediate disable; session/service-token revocation; dormant-account expiry; authoritative HR/admin linkage and periodic access recertification. **Built 2026-10-27 (next 20, erp-d3):** sign-ins are recorded when a full session is issued (password / MFA / store PIN, never purpose tokens), best effort; re-enabling gives a fresh window. Setting `security.dormant_account_days` (Off / 60-365, default **Off**): an hourly sweep disables accounts idle that long the way Deactivate does (session dropped, audit row) - never Super Admin, the support user or a client's last active admin, operational clients only; the Security tab lists accounts with no sign-in; the Users list shows Last sign-in. Migration `db/migrations_stage49_2_1_identity_lifecycle.sql` (two columns). `TestStage4921*` (4 engines, 1 server). Found, not fixed: saving an Active Employee re-activates its linked user, undoing a disable. Left: joiner / mover / leaver approval, HR linkage, recertification. [decided 2026-11-12: yes, see below] **Ask 164 (erp-0c, 2026-11-12):** Saving an Employee no longer re-activates a disabled user (`hr.go`, `handlers_core_doc_engine.go`); a leaving Employee disables its linked user (`identity_leaver.go`); joiner and mover changes go through maker-checker behind `SettingIdentityChangeApproval` (Off by default; a leaver stays immediate); Security tab Access review lists users with Confirm / Revoke (`identity_access_review.go`, `view-security.js`, migrations `49_2_1b` / `49_2_1c`, the second seeds `tenant_default` only). `TestStage4921*` pass; the leaver test was red first; panel checked in Chromium. Decided (user, 2026-11-12, "take it all recommned"): (a) a store switch counts as activity - already recorded (`handlers_store_access.go`, 49.2.1a); (b) a role change is a mover event needing approval behind the Off setting - no code path changes a user's role today (set only at creation), so any future role-change control must file a mover request when `security.identity_change_approval` is On. Left: the Users screen has no store-move control (only the `users/location` API) - item 164.3. Found and fixed: `49_2_1c` seeded the record type and its approval rule into the default client only, so an existing client with the setting On would have every joiner / mover refused; `migrations_stage49_2_1d_access_change_request_every_client` adds them to every client (run twice on a scratch DB with one client emptied: restored, then unchanged). **Ask 166 (erp-10):** 164.3 built (store move from the Users screen, both setting states checked in a browser). *Status: Done*
  - [x] **49.2.2 Password baseline.** Long passphrases, breached/common-password rejection using a privacy-preserving bounded method, modern adaptive password hashing with version/cost migration, constant-time comparisons, no arbitrary periodic reset, no recoverable password and safe rate-limited change flow. — *2026-09-08: `engines/password_policy.go` + embedded `engines/common_passwords.txt` (~300 curated entries incl. India-corporate `Word@123` patterns) is the one choke point `handleChangePassword`, `CompletePasswordReset` and `handleCreateUser` all call now — length (new `security.password_min_length` setting, default 12, Stage 30.7 registry pattern), denylist match, username-equality and a cheap ascending/descending/all-same-character sequence check. **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed**: `handleLogin`'s bcrypt check had a plaintext fallback (`u.PasswordHash != req.Password`) left over from old local-seed configs — every real seed is already a bcrypt hash so it had no legitimate caller, but its failure mode was backwards for a security control (a `password_hash` value that was ever plaintext, from a bad migration or manual SQL fix, would authenticate via a non-constant-time `!=`); removed, bcrypt is now the only accepted form. Added bcrypt cost-migration (`handleLogin` re-hashes at `bcrypt.DefaultCost` on successful login if the stored hash's cost is lower — best-effort, does not bump credential_version since it's not a real credential change). "No arbitrary periodic reset" and "no recoverable password" were already true and are now fully true with the fallback gone. **Also closes the credential_version half of 49.2.4** — see that item.*

MFA-change reauthentication was already true: the only self-service MFA mutation, `/me/mfa/reenroll`, already requires the account password (Stage 32.5); there is still no self-service "disable MFA" endpoint at all. **Auditable dual-control helpdesk recovery for privileged users** — the actual missing piece: there was no admin-driven "reset another user's password" endpoint at all before this. An ordinary target is reset immediately, in one call, the same single-admin shape `handleAdminResetUserMFA` already uses for MFA — a one-time 20-character random password (same unambiguous alphabet as MFA recovery codes) is returned once, credential_version bumps (revoking every existing session), and `SendPasswordChangedNotice` fires. **A Super Admin target instead requires a second, different Super Admin to approve** via the existing generic `/api/v1/approval/submit`+`/decide` (maker-checker already refuses the same actor as both requester and approver); the reset does not execute until Approved, and the generated password is surfaced **only in the approver's own decide response** (`handleDecideApproval` gained one doctype branch, since this is the one approval hook in the codebase whose result may not reach the requester — every other doctype's hook lives inside `engines.DecideApproval` itself and returns nothing to the HTTP layer). A reason is mandatory once the target is privileged, optional otherwise. An admin cannot use this endpoint to reset their own password (points to My Profile instead). The map was bootstrapped mechanically (moduleGate name, or `/admin/<segment>`, or the first `/api/v1/` path segment) rather than hand-typed per route — 454 routes made individual review impractical, a systematic rule is more auditable than 454 separate judgment calls; every `/api/v1/admin/*` route became `LevelAdmin` (Super-Admin-only), which changes no legitimate behavior since routes.go's own repeated "HR/Admin-only, enforced in each handler" comments already claimed this was already true — now actually enforced in one place. **Real bug found and fixed while wiring this in**: the first working version used `http.DefaultServeMux.Handler(r)` to recover the matched pattern, which is empty in every unit test (`Run()` — the only code that registers routes.go onto `DefaultServeMux` — is never called by a test, it blocks on `ListenAndServe`), so every handler-level test started failing 403 across the board. Fixed with a dedicated `capabilityLookupMux` built once from `routeCapabilities`' own keys (see that var's comment) — works identically whether a real server or a direct `apiMiddleware(handler)` test call. **Second, pre-existing bug found (not fixed, out of scope for this item)**: `document_numbering_api_test.go`'s update call built `/api/v1/doc/RFQ/`+firstID with the server-issued number's own `/`-separated form (`engines/numbering.go`'s default separator) left un-encoded — Go's real mux only matches `{id}` against an *escaped* slash (verified empirically: a raw `/` in the path does not match `/api/v1/doc/{doctype}/{id}` at all, an encoded `%2F` does). Fixed the test (`url.PathEscape(firstID)`, matching what a real client must send). **This same gap likely affects production today**, independent of this session's work: `public/app.js:18858`'s real edit-save call (`` `/api/v1/doc/${currentDoctype}/${editingDocID}` ``) does not encode the id either, and every numbered doctype (`engines/document_numbering.go`'s 11-doctype list — PurchaseOrder, GRN, ASN, RFQ, VendorQuote, TransferOrder, ExpenseClaim, Leave, EmployeeLoan, Grievance, ProductionOrder, Attendance) gets a `/`-separated series number by default — so editing one of these via the path-addressed route may already 404 silently, unrelated to authorization. A `NULL` location/owner is not global visibility unless the role has an explicit global capability. Add cross-entity/location/owner and employee-self negative tests. **Built 2026-09-05:** `engines/scope_policy.go` + wiring in `handlers_core_doc_engine.go`. The old single inline clause (`AND (COALESCE(data->>'location', data->>'location_code') = $n OR … IS NULL)`, applied to every doctype for every non-Super-Admin) conflated three different situations; the registry now separates them, keyed off each doctype's own `mandatory` flag in its `doctype_fields` seed: **(1)** no location field at all → unscoped, visible (MarketplaceSettlement/LogisticsBooking — the original comment was right); **(2)** location declared *optional* (19 doctypes: Printer, Zone, ShiftAssignment, TaskCompletionLog…) → an attribute, not a scope, so a missing value stays visible but a present one is still filtered; **(3)** location declared *mandatory* (26 doctypes: POSSession, GRN, PurchaseOrder, Wave, WarehouseTask…) → a missing value is an anomaly and is now **denied**, which is the item's "a NULL location is not global visibility" case. Two dimensions the old clause had no notion of were added: **self** (11 `employee_id` doctypes — a Cashier/Store Manager holding only *read* is confined to its own employee record; a role holding create/update on a doctype it administers for the team is exempt, listed explicitly in `selfScopeExemptions` from the shipped seed's own grants) and **owner** (4 mandatory-`owner_id` 3PL doctypes, gated on a new `scope.owner.global` capability; per-session owner scope itself is 47.5's work, so the capability is granted to Store Supervisor/Administrator today and 47.5 replaces the default rather than inventing the mechanism). Fails **closed**: a session whose scope value cannot be resolved (no linked `Employee` record, no owner) matches no row rather than every row. **A real client-side-filtering leak found and closed while doing this**: `renderMyRequestsTab` (`public/app.js:14700-14707`) fetches the *whole* of `/api/v1/doc/Leave`, `/ExpenseClaim` and `/Grievance` and filters by `l.employee_id === employee.code` **in the browser** — every colleague's leave reasons, expense amounts and grievance descriptions were already on the wire; server-side self scope closes it with the page rendering exactly the same rows. **Legal entity is deliberately NOT a dimension yet, on measured evidence**: `Location.legal_entity` is optional and populated on 1 of 112 rows, `JournalVoucher.entity` optional and populated on **0 of 199**, and `StatusTransitionRule.entity` — the only mandatory "entity" field in the schema — does not mean a legal entity at all (its own Select options are `Order,OrderLine,FulfillmentOrder,Shipment`; stored values are doctype names like `ExpenseClaim`), so scoping on it would be wrong, not merely strict. `ScopeDimension` names it so 47.12.2/47.12.3 extend this file. A doctype the registry does not know (one built at runtime through the Doctype Builder) keeps exactly the pre-47.1.4 clause. **Cost, per 47.18's ledger rule**: the only new per-request work is the self dimension's Employee lookup, so it is resolved lazily — on GET only, and only for a doctype that declares a self field (a write to Payslip/Leave pays nothing) — and `db/migrations_stage47_1_role_templates.sql` adds the partial index `documents((data->>'user_id')) WHERE doctype='Employee'` that this lookup shape (shared with the pre-existing `GET /api/v1/hr/my-employee`) never had. Location and owner add no query at all: they are extra `AND` predicates on the list query that was already running. 8 tests in `engines/scope_policy_test.go`, including a completeness test that re-parses `db/*.sql` and fails the build if the registry drifts from the seeds.

### SEC › Security hardening & findings

- [ ] **26.1.3b** Activate the Cloudflare edge WAF — managed rules, L7 DDoS absorption and bot controls. The domain and zone now exist (`wholeops.in`, 2026-08-14), so the external prerequisite is met; both records are still grey-cloud.
  - **⚠️ Correction (2026-08-15): "pure activation" was wrong, and the reason is worth keeping.** The plan of record was orange-cloud + CIDRs in two places + lock the origin. That combination **breaks Let's Encrypt renewal on this box**: once the origin firewall admits only Cloudflare, ACME cannot reach it — TLS-ALPN-01 cannot work through a proxy that terminates TLS, and HTTP-01 through the Cloudflare proxy is documented as unreliable. Nothing would have failed on the day; `app.wholeops.in`'s certificate would simply have stopped renewing and expired on **2026-11-12**, with the per-tenant on-demand path breaking sooner. DNS-01 is the usual escape and is refused here for the reason 26.1.3a already gave: it needs a provider plugin, therefore `xcaddy`, therefore a custom Caddy binary to keep patched.
  - **Resolution (user decision, 2026-08-15): a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate** — free, 15-year, wildcard, issued by Cloudflare and trusted only by Cloudflare, which is exactly the CF↔origin hop. No ACME, no plugin, no renewal, and it is what makes a fully locked-down origin possible rather than merely aspirational.
  - **Repository work for this is DONE (2026-08-15) and was not previously counted:** `deploy/enable_tls.sh` gained a `--cloudflare --origin-cert --origin-key` mode and `deploy/Caddyfile` was parameterised so **one** file describes both postures instead of two files drifting. `--cloudflare` fetches Cloudflare's published CIDRs at install time (never hardcoded — a stale list silently mis-attributes client IPs), **fails closed if the fetch fails** rather than installing a partial trust list, writes them into *both* Caddy's `trusted_proxies` (with `trusted_proxies_strict`) *and* `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` in `/etc/erp/erp.env`, and restarts the app. One command owns both halves because their drift is silent: an unlisted edge node is mistaken for the client and every visitor collapses into one rate-limit bucket. It also **inverts the DNS pre-flight check** instead of skipping it — in `--cloudflare` mode the name must resolve *into* a Cloudflare range, because installing an Origin CA certificate while the record is still grey-cloud serves browsers a certificate only Cloudflare trusts and takes the site down.
  - **Still open — the parts that are genuinely portal-side:** orange-cloud both records, SSL/TLS mode → Full (strict), generate the Origin CA certificate, enable the free managed WAF ruleset + Bot Fight Mode + Authenticated Origin Pulls, a rate-limiting rule on `/api/v1/login`, a cache-bypass rule (Cloudflare caches `.js`/`.css` by default and `public/app.js` has no content hash in its name, so a deploy would otherwise serve stale JS), and restricting inbound 80/443 in the **DigitalOcean cloud firewall** — not just `ufw`, which was never what was blocking traffic here (44.9).
- [ ] **47.8 Session containment and XSS-resistant browser boundary** — closes A-08.
  - [x] **47.8.1** Inventory every inline handler/dynamic HTML insertion and classify safe text, trusted template or user/external data. Add shared escaping/sanitization at the narrowest choke point. — *2026-10-03: added a source-count inventory and escaped high-risk PIM/report/log/import and fixed-asset/printer renderers; all-source classification remains open. See `docs/security/browser-boundary-inventory.md`.* — *2026-10-04: inventory now complete and reproducible; classification deliberately reported as **incomplete**. The inline-handler half is finished (see 47.8.2): all 126 attributes enumerated, classified by shape and migrated. The HTML-sink half: **461** insertion sinks hold **637** interpolated expressions, mechanically classified as 192 escaped at the sink, 115 with no interpolation, 102 trusted/config/assembled markup, 43 markup loops, and **300 candidates flagged for review**. The 300 is a candidate list, not a defect count: a systematic 16-item hand sample (every ~19th finding) found **6 genuine or needs-a-human-call and 10 false positives** (fixed colour values, fixed labels, counts, markup helpers, assembled-markup locals, and nested templates whose inner interpolation *is* escaped), so the real population is on the order of 110. Sound separation of trusted config (`t.label`) from untrusted data (`line.sku`) needs data-flow analysis the classifier does not do, and a wrong classification is worse than an acknowledged unknown — which is why this stays open rather than being declared resolved. Full artifact committed at `docs/security/browser-boundary-sink-classification.json` so the next pass starts from it instead of re-deriving it; per-file distribution in `docs/security/browser-boundary-inventory.md` §3. **Shared choke points improved**: `actionAttrs` now owns escaping for every action argument (previously an unescaped JavaScript source position), and `cfgEsc` — a byte-for-byte duplicate of `escapeHTMLText` with 55 call sites across five modules, where an escape-rule change would have had to be made twice — is now a delegating alias, so there is one escaping implementation. Renaming the 55 callers is a mechanical follow-up with no behavioural content. See `project_ledger.md` §190.* *Closed by Stage 134.8 (2026-10-23); ticked in the 2026-10-25 build-all run.*
  - [x] **47.8.2** Replace inline handlers with event delegation/module listeners and adopt nonce/hash CSP without `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`; enforce in report-only mode first, then blocking. — *2026-10-03: strict same-origin candidate now reports violations to a bounded same-origin handler; targeted Log Hub, report, generic-record and asset row actions moved to listeners/data attributes. More than 90 handler locations remain; enforcing CSP stays unsafe.* — *2026-10-04: **DONE.** All **126** inline event-handler attributes removed — 109 across `app.js` and the 18 view modules, 17 in `index.html` — replaced by one document-level delegated dispatcher (`handleDelegatedAction`) driven by `data-act`/`data-act-args` that `actionAttrs()` emits. Converted mechanically after classifying every handler first: 107 of 109 were a single function call, and the two that were not became named functions (`goToDefaultView`, `selectFieldText`) because the dispatcher calls one function per action and has no statement sequencing — sequencing would mean evaluating source, which is the thing being removed. **This was a security fix, not only a CSP enabler**: an inline handler interpolated its arguments as JavaScript source inside an HTML attribute, so a record id, SKU or doctype containing a quote closed the attribute and the string and executed whatever followed — a live injection sink wherever that value came from imported data, a connector payload, a report row or a CSV cell. Arguments now travel JSON-encoded and HTML-escaped, so a hostile value can only arrive as data. **The new harness found a latent primitive in the dispatcher itself**: `window['eval']` is a function, so a hostile `data-act` name with a string argument would have been arbitrary code execution. `resolveActionFunction` now requires a plain identifier that is an **own** property of `window` (excluding `constructor`/`__proto__`/`toString`) and is not a native built-in — an allowlist by construction rather than a denylist chasing the platform. Not reachable today (every name is a string literal in this repository), so defence in depth rather than a patched hole. **CSP flipped report-only -> blocking**, both phases measured in real Chromium across all 29 dispatched views: the strict candidate reported **zero** violations of any directive *before* it was enforced (that is what authorised the flip), and after enforcing `script-src 'self'` — zero enforced violations, zero `script-src` violations, zero page errors, re-confirmed a third time after the dispatcher hardening. **No nonce and no hash list, deliberately**: there are no inline `<script>` blocks for a nonce to permit (all six scripts are external files), a nonce would add a per-request value that defeats static caching of `index.html`, and `script-src` nonces do not whitelist handler *attributes* anyway — that needs `'unsafe-hashes'`, a weaker position than removing them. The report-only header now carries the next candidate, strict `style-src`, whose 629 inline-style violations are visible but not enforced, so the same two-phase mechanism is in place for that step. All **128** `data-act` reference sites were also checked statically against every way this codebase publishes a function to `window`; all resolve except four pre-existing dangling names in dead `index.html` modal markup (reported, not fixed — removing those overlays is a UI decision). `TestSecurityHeadersEnforcesStrictScriptSrc` now guards the inverse of what it used to: that the exception is not quietly reintroduced. See `project_ledger.md` §190.*

  - [x] **49.4.4 Shared request envelope.** Allowlisted method/content type/encoding; bounded headers/body/multipart/decompression/JSON depth/array/string/numeric range; strict duplicate/ambiguous parameter handling; deadlines/cancellation; safe error catalog and correlation ID with no stack/SQL/path/secret leak.
    *Built 2026-10-09 (five-item pass, item 3), to the scope the user set.*
    - **Where:** new `internal/server/request_envelope.go`, called once from `apiMiddleware`
      and from `publicAPIMiddleware` (the second caller boundary). It replaces the bare 2 MB
      `MaxBytesReader`.
    - **Limits** (in ADMIN_GUIDE §D's table): methods allowlisted (405 `GLOBAL-0303`). JSON or
      multipart only, plus per-route CSV/CSP types, and compressed bodies refused (415
      `GLOBAL-0304`). Bodies 2 MB, or 10/16/100/5 MB on the upload and import routes (413
      `GLOBAL-0305`). Headers 100 fields/32 KB (431 `GLOBAL-0306`). JSON is streamed and checked
      for depth 32, lists of 50,000, strings of 1 MB (2 MB for StickerTemplate), exact duplicate
      keys including escape-spelled ones, and trailing values (400 `GLOBAL-0307`). A 55 s
      deadline flows through `r.Context()`; an expired one answers 504 `GLOBAL-0308` instead of
      `writeEngineError`'s empty 499.
    - **Measured first:** stored documents reach depth 3, arrays of 7 and strings of 654. A
      sticker logo is ≤400 KB, about 546 KB as a data URL, and all of a design's logos share the
      one `design` string. `max_import_rows` is 20,000. Measuring found that the old 2 MB cap
      silently overrode every larger per-handler limit, because a nested `MaxBytesReader` cannot
      raise an outer one: media upload, imports and the hook were all really capped at 2 MB.
    - **Decisions:** case-variant keys are allowed, because `copies_override`, `dispositions` and
      `exchange_for` are SKU/line-keyed maps. A body with no `Content-Type` is read as JSON and
      fully bounded; the allowlist's real job is blocking preflight-free `text/plain`/form
      bodies. Rejections are not logged (log-flood risk).
    - **Tests:** `request_envelope_test.go` has one case per limit through `apiMiddleware`
      (status, code, matching correlation id, no leak). Large legitimate payloads pass: a
      3.0 MB 20,000-row multipart CSV, a 1.6 MB three-logo sticker design and a 2,000-line PO;
      the first two are refused on a default route. Plus deadline-vs-abandoned.
    - **Live on scratch :8147:**
      - a real three-logo StickerTemplate save returned 200;
      - a real 3.4 MB/20,000-row import preview passed the envelope;
      - dup key → 400, TRACE → 405, text/plain → 415, each with its correlation id;
      - a Playwright sweep of all 53 lazy views + 4 setup screens made 341 API calls with zero
        envelope rejections.
    - **Not done (follow-up 49.4.4a):** numeric range bounds and duplicate query-parameter
      handling, which the user's scope did not include.

      per-row lookups in the preview or move large previews onto the async job runner.
      — *Done 2026-10-11, synchronously (no job runner needed).* **Profiled first:** on any tenant
      but `tenant_default` it was ~17 ms/row, about 20 round trips per Item row. `GetSettingInt`
      ran once per non-empty field, each re-resolving the tenant schema. On top of that, every row
      re-read the doctype meta, field permissions and the Master flag, did an EXISTS, then an
      upsert. **Fix, in the shared core only** (`runDocDataImportContext`/`importBatch`, so plain
      CSV, PIM preview and templated import all get it): `importLookups` resolves meta, the
      length setting, permissions and the Master flag once per file. Each batch prefetches its Link
      targets with one `id = ANY` query per Link field (self-links stay per row) and writes its
      valid rows in one `unnest` upsert whose `RETURNING (xmax = 0)` gives the create/update
      split. `ValidateDocument` became a wrapper over `validateDocumentWith`, and now reads
      `field_max_length` once per call rather than once per field, which also helps every
      single-record save.
      **Found on the way and fixed in the same statement:** the import upsert had the
      cross-record-type overwrite `7657453` fixed for generic create. An Item row whose code was a
      Brand's id overwrote the Brand's data and reported "created". The import now refuses that
      row. **Measured, same 20,000-row fixture, scratch tenant:** preview 6 m 0.6 s → **5.2 s**;
      import → **4.4 s**. **Live** on scratch :8147 (tenant_default): 20,000-row
      `POST /pim/import/Item/preview` **200 in 6.9 s**, 0 rows written. **Tests:**
      `engines/import_perf_test.go`, both A/B-confirmed to fail on HEAD:
      `TestItemImportPreviewFitsTheDeadlineAtTheRowCap` (must stay under 27.5 s, half the
      deadline) and `TestBatchedImportKeepsPerRowAnswers` (1,203 rows over 3 batches: Link
      found/missing/Cancelled, file-order ids, re-preview = all updates with no write,
      cross-type refusal, restricted fields).
      **Still per row:** a row with no id/code draws its sequence number with one query. Every
      Master import (Item, Vendor, Customer…) carries a code, so it isn't affected. Guides:
      USER_GUIDE §8d.5 large-file note, ADMIN_GUIDE §D.
    - [x] (c) The Extension Hook Log view calls `GET /api/v1/admin/extension/hooks/log`, which is

      100 calls for this hook"). Its id lived only in memory, set by View Log, so a cold open or

      `/hooks/log`, a 404. `public/view-admin.js` now keeps the chosen hook for the tab

      the empty state with no API error; View Log got 200 from the real route; a reload kept the

      errors. *Not fixed, noted:* a non-UUID id makes the handler answer 500 (a uuid cast error)

    - [x] (a) *Done 2026-10-11.* **Numbers** (measured: the 29,571 JSON numbers stored in both
      local DBs peak at 5,000,000, with literals of at most 18 characters):
      `validateJSONEnvelope` now refuses |x| > 2^53−1, the largest integer a float64 handler and
      the browser hold exactly (about 700× the largest real class, paise and epoch ms), and
      literals over 64 characters. The only inbound int64 decoder (the BigCommerce webhook's order
      id) carries small sequential ids. **Duplicate query parameters, model approved by the user
      2026-10-11 ("Reject all"):** any repeated name → 400 `GLOBAL-0307`. Names are compared after
      URL-decoding, case-sensitively, and an empty repeat counts. The raw query is split by hand,
      because `url.ParseQuery` drops a pair whose value fails to decode. A route that needs a list
      declares it in `envelopeRoutes.repeatableParams`; none does today. Every one of the 131 server
      reads is first-value `.Get()`, and the frontend never repeats a name. **Tests:**

      zero page errors. ADMIN_GUIDE §D limits table updated.

Also lists **known-and-accepted items not to re-report** (e.g. `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`, which is deliberate given the no-build-step frontend) so engagement budget isn't spent rediscovering documented decisions, and puts DoS/volumetric testing explicitly out of scope since 26.11.2 already measured load and a stress test on the single shared droplet would buy an outage rather than a finding.

## PLAT — Platform & Workflow

### PLAT › Background jobs, events & notifications

- [ ] **47.11 Graceful request control, worker ownership and side-effect safety** — closes A-10/A-11/A-15/A-16/A-17/A-44; extends rather than duplicates Stage 38.6.
  - [x] **47.11.1** Bound/prune rate-limit and tenant-concurrency state; define replica-consistent semantics using PostgreSQL only where shared state is required. Unique invalid keys cannot grow memory indefinitely.
    — *Done 2026-10-11.* **Measured first** (200,000 requests each, old code): rotating IPs left
    200,000 limiter keys (+22.2 MB), and rotating junk bearer tokens from one IP left 200,000
    keys (+23.8 MB). None were ever freed, and the junk-token run was **never refused**: each junk
    token got its own fresh budget, a full per-IP limit bypass. The tenant in-flight map kept
    every tenant id it had seen at 0, and before auth that id can come from `X-Tenant-ID` or
    `?tenant_id=`. **Fix, `internal/server/middleware.go` only:**
    - `sessionFingerprint` gives a per-session bucket only to a token that passes
      `engines.ParseToken` (signature + expiry, in-memory keyring, no DB), so junk shares the
      address's budget.
    - `RateLimiter` sweeps keys idle past the window (at most every 10 s) and caps its table at
      100,000 keys (~11 MB). A new key that finds it full, after a forced sweep at most once a
      second, counts in a shared overflow bucket per limit. Known callers keep their own budget,
      and overflow is logged once per sweep.
    - `tenantConcurrencyLimiter.release` deletes a tenant at 0.

    **Replica semantics** (written in the code and ADMIN_GUIDE §D): both are per process by
    design. Behind N replicas a caller can draw up to N× a budget, and the tenant cap protects
    each process's own pool. State that must be shared is already in PostgreSQL (account lockout
    `users.failed_login_count`/`locked_until`, MFA/reset tokens, `credential_version`), so no new
    shared table was needed. **Tests** (`internal/server/rate_limit_bounds_test.go`): 300,000
    rotating keys → 100,001 keys / +11.1 MB; sweep after the window; a full table keeps an
    existing 5/min budget exact; 5,000 junk tenants leave an empty map with the cap intact;
    through `apiMiddleware`, junk tokens hit the address's budget on request 101 and two signed
    sessions behind one address keep separate budgets (Stage 30.5.9). The junk-token and tenant
    tests are A/B-confirmed to fail on HEAD.
  - [x] **47.11.2** Coalesce identical browser requests, cancel stale view work, define per-view initial-call budgets and return a specific retryable overload response/`Retry-After` instead of generic `GLOBAL-0010` for real overload. **Built 2026-10-25 (build-all, 20 items):** identical reads in flight share one request (`apiFetch`, each caller gets its own copy); the per-session limiter's 429 now carries `Retry-After` (`RateLimiter.RetryAfter`, the oldest hit's exit from the window), and a read told to wait ≤5 s waits once and goes again quietly. **Verified:** `TestRateLimiterRetryAfterIsTheOldestHitsExit`; Chromium - two simultaneous reads, one request, both answers readable. **Left:** cancelling stale view work and per-view initial-call budgets. Keep `[ ]`. **Built 2026-10-30 (erp-6b, next 20):** stale view work: `renderView` opens an `AbortController` per view; reads begun during that render are aborted on the next switch and dropped quietly (no stray timeout / connection alert). Per-view initial-call budget (`VIEW_READ_BUDGET`: default 10, list 12, home / POS 16, reports 14) with a console warning on localhost / staging only. Checked in Chromium on a scratch server. See 162.5. *Status: Done*

### PLAT › Database, migrations & data integrity

- [ ] **51.1a** Decide and run a production data backfill for Master records already stranded with `id != code` (created before 51.1 shipped). Needs a read-only audit query against prod first (how many rows, which doctypes) before deciding an UPDATE approach — do not run against production without explicit sign-off given it mutates live primary-key-equivalent values. **[needs design decision: user — backfill approach and timing, likely alongside the batched deploy]** — *2026-10-04: **the audit half is done; the decision is still the open part.** Decision taken this pass (user): write the read-only audit query only, no UPDATE authored yet. Delivered `scripts/audit_master_id_code_drift.sql`, six sections: (1) stranded rows per doctype with date range, (2) stranded/healthy/no-code totals so the ratio is visible, (3) a **collision check that must be empty before any backfill** — rows whose wanted id is already occupied by a different record, i.e. two records claiming one identity, which no plain UPDATE can resolve, (4) a **blast-radius** count of transactions already referencing each stray id, since an UPDATE that moves a Master id without rewriting those trades one broken reference for another on documents that currently work, (5) a 50-row sample to eyeball, (6) a per-tenant headline so it is clear whether `minn` is the only affected tenant. Deliberately placed in `scripts/`, **not** `db/` — `db/migrate.go:361` executes every `.sql` file in that directory, so this must never sit there. Pass the schema with `-v schema=...` (defaults to `tenant_minn`); the in-file default is `\if :{?schema}`-guarded so the command-line override actually wins. **Validated end-to-end against the local dev DB**: ran clean and found 12 stranded rows of 503 Master rows in `tenant_default` (Item ×4, plus Brand/CartonType/Channel/DockDoor/HoldCode/ReasonCode/Trailer/Zone), **zero collisions**, and 10 transactions already pointing at stray ids (9 for Item, 1 for ReasonCode). Nothing was written and **no production connection was made from here** — running it against prod, and the backfill decision that follows, are still the user's. Section 6 was first written as a `DO` block using `RAISE NOTICE`; that lands on stderr and interleaved unpredictably with the result sets when redirected to a file, so it was rebuilt with `\gexec`. Note the dev-DB numbers are not a prediction of prod's — prod has months of real master data created before 51.1 shipped. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §184.*

BLD-046 itself still stays open — this only resolved the mechanical manifest-drift blocker, not the item's own real, unaddressed "interrupted cases" gap (abandoned-request cancellation) described above.* — *2026-10-02: BLD-041 (lazy screen code and initial payload budgets) opened — real progress, stays open; first session to touch it. **Baseline measured** (3 real cold-cache HTTP runs against a disposable scratch server, `Accept-Encoding: gzip`, consistent across all 3): cold-core (index.html + styles.css + db.js + components/erp-typeahead.js + app.js + qz-print.js) = 347,586 bytes (~339.4 KiB) vs. the 180 KiB budget; initial JS alone = 307,602 bytes (~300.4 KiB) vs. the 120 KiB budget — app.js alone is ~96.7% of the JS total and the only real lever. **Approach decided**: native lazy loading via a plain dynamically-injected `<script src="...">` per view group, NOT an ES-module/`import()` rewrite — app.js stays one classic script, so every function/let/const a chunk declares at its top level becomes global exactly as it would if it still lived in app.js (classic `<script>` tags on one page share one global function/var and top-level `let`/`const` environment), meaning zero import/export wiring at any of its ~600 existing cross-references. New shared `loadViewChunk(src)` helper (dedup `Set` + one `<script>` element + onload/onerror) lets `renderViewContent`'s existing per-view dispatch branches `await` a chunk before calling its render function — fits the already-existing scratch-buffer-then-atomic-swap rendering pipeline with no changes to it, and a load failure renders the same `renderErrorPanel`-with-retry every other load failure in this app uses (not a crash, not silently skipped). **Mechanism proven end-to-end on 4 real, independently-verified view groups**, each confirmed self-contained first by grepping every identifier it declares across the whole ~24,500-line file (zero inbound references other than the one dispatcher call, zero outbound references to anything but shared/core helpers) before moving it: Manufacturing (`view-manufacturing.js`, ~570 lines, incl. its own `currentMfgTab`/`MFG_TABS` module state), Expenses (`view-expenses.js`), Fixed Assets (`view-assets.js`), and Stock Transfer (`view-transfers.js`, incl. its own `transferLineItems` state and its reads/writes of the shared core `state.docData` scratch array). Live-verified with Playwright for all 4: a cold load that never visits any of the four fetches 0 bytes of any of them; the first visit to each fetches its chunk exactly once; a second visit (even after navigating away) is served from cache with no re-fetch; every screen's tabs/actions/sub-views render and respond correctly (incl. Manufacturing's MRP tab and its Quality tab's delegation into the shared, still-core `renderDocTableView`); a simulated chunk-load failure on a fresh page (`route.abort`) shows the retry panel with zero uncaught page errors, and a subsequent real load succeeds. **Measured result**: cold-core dropped 347,586 → 337,701 bytes (~9.9 KB, ~2.8%) and initial JS 307,602 → 297,717 bytes across these 4 of ~56 total dispatched views — a small but real, fully mechanical, fully verified dent; closing the budget gap needs this same proven pattern applied to most of the remaining ~52 views, which is real, scoped, repeatable follow-up (same shape as the `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` rollout's own 3-session mechanical scale-out), not a one-session undertaking. `node --check`/`go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (after two `attack_surface.json` regenerations for the new static files — `go run ./cmd/surfacescan -out %TEMP%\...` then copied in via PowerShell, no permission block this time); `graphify update .` and `pwsh docs/brain/update-brain.ps1` run after (the latter needed one `brain.map.json` edit: added a `public/view-*.js` glob to the existing "SPA Shell" region so future chunk files auto-claim without a further edit). Files touched: `public/app.js`, four new `public/view-*.js` chunk files, `docs/security/attack_surface.json`, `docs/brain/{brain.map.json,BRAIN.md,brain.html}`, `docs/generated/brain-manifest.json`. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §180, build checklist BLD-041 entry.* — *Correction, later 2026-10-02: the dynamic-`<script>` `loadViewChunk` mechanism above was superseded.
