Deliberately excludes every Settings/admin screen from the tile grid — the retired 2026-08-01 Dashboard's own failure mode. Live-verified with real Playwright across all 5 named personas (cashier, Accounts Payable "buyer", Picker "floor operator", Accountant, tenant admin) plus Store Supervisor, each reaching its main task in one click; Picker (most permission-limited) shows zero unauthorized/disabled tiles; a freshly-provisioned empty single-package tenant confirmed real module-gating and the setup-guidance/no-Recent states. `USER_GUIDE.md`/`USER_SOP.md`/`UAT_CHECKLIST.md`/KB updated (all previously said Reports is the landing screen). BLD-034–040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §166, build checklist BLD-033 entry.* — *2026-09-25: BLD-038 (accessibility and localization acceptance) built and verified. Found and fixed a real, app-wide gap: every `.modal-overlay` dialog hides via `opacity:0`, not `display:none`, so a closed dialog stayed fully reachable by Tab and exposed to the accessibility tree — a 120-press Tab walk from Home hit 53 invisible controls belonging to closed dialogs. Fixed via synchronous `inert` toggling at each real open/close pair (a first MutationObserver-based attempt was live-tested and found to race a synchronous `.focus()` call in `openFieldModal`, so it was replaced, not patched over). Also found and fixed: screen-reader announcements were almost entirely absent app-wide (one `aria-live` region existed in the whole app) — `showToast`/`renderPageBanner`/the shared custom-dialog markup now announce; Home's new approvals stat-card (plus two pre-existing tiles of the same shape — exec-dashboard, OMS) was a plain unreachable `<div>` with a click handler, fixed via a new shared `makeClickable()` helper; the new `.stat-card[role="button"]`/`.home-recent-item` focus rings were invisible under Windows High Contrast (`forced-colors`) since `box-shadow` is not repainted there — fixed by pairing them with a real `outline`. Contrast (text/non-text, both themes), non-color status meaning, target size, 320px reflow, text-spacing (1.4.12) and reduced-motion all came back clean on live measurement. Locale/RTL: no i18n/RTL infrastructure exists in this app (by design, not an oversight) — scoped smoke tests only, no unsupported language coverage claimed. BLD-034–037/039/040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §168, build checklist BLD-038 entry.* — *2026-09-27: BLD-037 (coherent visual hierarchy and support context) built and verified. Four real findings, all fixed at a shared choke point: the environment/support banner truncated to one ellipsized line with the rest reachable only via hover, silently hiding 3 of its 4 real operational notices on any touch device — fixed with a real "Show more"/"Show less" toggle and JS-computed body padding; Home's lone pending-approvals stat-card stretched to the full shared-grid row width and read as a half-empty panel — capped via a scoped `.home-stats-row` override (same precedent as `.oms-tile`); the backend's own promised "correlation_id shown to the user" was never actually read off the error envelope by the frontend — now surfaced on the modal and Page banner (152/302 catalog rows) error paths; every generic doctype-table screen's raw PascalCase identifier ("PurchaseOrder", "SalesInvoice") and a stray placeholder subtitle ("Pluggable module metadata records database") leaked to real users app-wide (~90 Setup-sidebar entries, verified live with zero misfires) — fixed via a new `humanizeIdentifier()`/`getDoctypeLabel()` pair applied only at doctype-heading call sites, deliberately not at `getTranslatedLabel` itself (a first attempt there was live-caught corrupting unrelated text like "3PL"→"3 PL" and reverted before shipping). Incidental bug found and fixed along the way: a bogus, wrongly-cased "← Back to home" breadcrumb on every cold doctype-table deep-link load. `go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1` run clean except the pre-existing, unrelated Stage 51.8 "Stores" module gap. BLD-036/039/040 remain open. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §169, build checklist BLD-037 entry.* — *2026-09-28: BLD-034 (usable dense tables) built and verified — the generic doctype-table screen used to fetch the server's already-capped 500-row default once and paginate/search that fixed batch client-side, so on BLD-043's seeded 250k-row tables every row past the first 500 was permanently unreachable and search could never see past that same window. Pagination/search/counts are now real server round trips (SQL-side search, opt-in `count=true` total via a new `X-Total-Count` header so the endpoint's other ~13 callers, Link-field typeaheads chief among them, don't pay for it); found and closed a field-permission search-oracle gap in the same pass, and found and fixed a genuinely pre-existing bug while verifying "persistent context" — a doctype-table refresh had never actually restored its page/search since Stage 41 (indistinguishable from a fresh deep link), now fixed narrowly so hint/bookmark links still land clean. Explicitly measured and flagged, not fixed: free-text search is correct at scale now but slow (~2s at 250k rows — the per-row JSON scan itself, not the query shape) — a real fix needs a trigram/GIN index, a separate decision. Live-verified with Playwright against the 250k-row seeded dataset; three fresh full-suite rounds green. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §172, build checklist BLD-034 entry.* — *2026-09-29: BLD-035 (consistent forms and truthful transaction outcomes) built and verified. The generic doc engine's create/update response hardcoded `{"status":"saved"}` regardless of the document's real resulting status — fixed to report the value actually persisted (including a re-approval-on-edit reset to Pending Approval); `handleDecideApproval` similarly only ever echoed the bare decision even though approving a VendorInvoice override finalizes it straight to Paid — added the row's real post-decision `document_status`. Wired five previously-silent call sites (generic form save, PO/generic/QualityInspection submit-for-approval, decideApproval, savePurchaseOrder's plain create/edit path) to show the real outcome via one new shared helper. Added missing `Date`/`Currency` fieldtype rendering to the generic form (dozens of real Date fields across many doctypes were falling into a plain text box with no date picker). **Found and fixed live, not originally scoped**: `editDocRecord`/`handleDynamicFormSubmit`/`deleteDocRecord` built their edit/delete URL with the document id un-encoded — since Stage 51.1 made `id` equal the human-readable `code` (e.g. `"Vendor/HQ/2026/000002"`), this 404'd Edit-load, Save and Delete for the great majority of real records; fixed to match the PO composer's own already-correct `encodeURIComponent` pattern. Live-verified end-to-end with real Playwright against a disposable Postgres/scratch-server instance; three fresh full-suite rounds green. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §175, build checklist BLD-035 entry.* — *2026-09-30: BLD-036 (recoverable empty/loading/error/offline states) worked — real progress, stays open. `apiFetch`/`apiUpload` gained a client-side timeout (30s/90s, honest wording that never claims a mutating call's server-side effect is known once the client gives up waiting); a new shared `guardAgainstDoubleSubmit` helper closes a real duplicate-document-creation gap on the generic record form and the PO composer (each POST gets a fresh server-numbered code, so a double-click could create two real documents); `renderApprovalsView`'s load failure now uses the existing `renderErrorPanel` retry instead of a dead-end panel or blank screen. Live-verified with real Playwright: exactly one POST fires for a synchronous double-submit on both forms; a normal save still completes correctly; the real 30s timeout, a hard connection failure, a real 409 conflict (server's actual message surfaces, edit preserved) and a native-validation block on an empty mandatory field all behave correctly with input never lost. One full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (no Go files touched) — notably the two Stage 51.8 "Stores" tests now pass on this tree, worth a follow-up check. **Stays open**: the double-submit guard covers two save paths, not every create/decide action app-wide; only 1 of ~54 "Failed to load" call sites got the retry treatment this pass; "stable loading layout" wasn't assessed. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §176, build checklist BLD-036 entry.* — *2026-09-30 (continued): worked all three named gaps in the same thread. Double-submit guard extended to `decideApproval`'s Approve/Reject buttons and the GRN workbench's "Post Receipt" (both newly guarded); POS checkout's manual disable was converted to the shared helper too, with its internal price-changed recursive retry repointed at the new unguarded inner function so re-entering the guard mid-retry doesn't no-op it. 13 more of the 54 "Failed to load" dead ends fixed on the five personas' critical paths (Fulfillment, Yard Board, WMS Cockpit, Chart of Accounts, Trial Balance, Purchase Orders list, OMS Orders, System Status, all four Log Hub tabs) — ~34 of 54 now have a working retry. "Stable loading layout" actually assessed: `renderView`'s scratch-buffer/atomic-swap navigation and the generic doctype-table's stale-while-revalidate pagination (BLD-034) are both already correct; several bespoke section-refresh functions (yard board, cockpit, OMS orders, similar WMS/Finance screens) still blank-then-fill instead, named as real follow-up. **Two real bugs found by live-verifying these fixes, not by reading them**: the Chart of Accounts retry closed over `renderView`'s off-screen scratch-buffer container (the exact bug class `renderView`'s own comment warns about) and silently rendered into a detached, removed node on retry - fixed by resolving the live parent at click time instead; the Log Hub jobs-pane retry left `jobsLoadFailed` (a `const`) stale after a successful reload, so the old failure message never cleared - fixed by making it `let` and reassigning it in `reloadJobs()`. Live-verified with real Playwright against the same disposable Postgres/`:8096` scratch server: busy-label + exactly-one-POST confirmed for `decideApproval`/`createGRN` under a forced race; POS checkout's guard/validation-restore mechanics confirmed (full price-change retry path not re-driven live, verified instead by `node --check`/full `go test`/manual review since the change was a mechanical extraction); 6 distinct retry surfaces confirmed via forced-500-then-succeed round trips, including the two bugs above confirmed fixed after the event. One full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green (no Go files touched). **Stays open, narrower now**: ~30 other bespoke create/save/submit composers (HR, Assets, Manufacturing, other WMS) remain unguarded; ~20 of the 54 dead-end sites are action-triggered fetches on otherwise-interactive screens, assessed but not individually re-verified; the named bespoke section-refresh screens still need the stale-while-revalidate retrofit. Left 1 real GRN + 1 rejected stale Item approval in `custom_erp_test` from verification (not rolled back - harmless in a disposable fixture). Full detail `project_ledger.md` §177, build checklist BLD-036 entry.* — *2026-10-01: gap (1) worked further — 18 more bespoke composers guarded app-wide (Admin, POS return, WMS Yard/Dock/Appointments, OMS, Procurement, HR incl. 3 self-service forms, Fixed Assets, Transfers, Expenses, Manufacturing), 23 save paths guarded in total. Live-verified exactly-one-POST-on-double-click across 4 representative modules (RFQ/BOM/Asset/YardCheckIn); full `go test` regression green. Found and named, not fixed (decision-sized, broad blast radius): a real timezone-skew bug in `AuditVerificationOverdue`/`audit_checkpoints.created_at` (server session timezone leaking into a naive `TIMESTAMP` column, misread as UTC by Go) — a third instance of a bug class already hit twice before. Full detail `project_ledger.md` §178, build checklist BLD-036 entry.* — *2026-10-02: gap (2) worked — all remaining ~28 action-triggered "Failed to load" sites individually re-verified (not just assessed), closing gap (2) for real. Found 4 already properly handled via `renderErrorPanel`+retry that an earlier session's grep undercounted (`loadDoctypeConfig`, `renderPrefixConfigsView`, `renderApprovalRulesView`, the doctype-table's own two schema/data-fetch sites) — no change needed there. Found and fixed one genuine, real dead end: `renderConfigurationView` (the admin Configuration settings page) showed only a one-time `showApiError` modal on failure with no persistent retry — once dismissed, the page was a bare header with no way back short of navigating away and back. Fixed with the same inline partial-section-message + "Try Again" pattern `renderSystemStatusView` already established for this exact shape (its header renders before the fetch, so a full `renderErrorPanel` takeover would wipe it). Live-verified with Playwright: forced 500 shows the real server message plus a persistent retry button with the header intact; retry on subsequent success reloads cleanly. All other ~27 sites confirmed non-dead-ends (each has a live re-trigger: a filter/date control, a tab bar, or the triggering button itself remains interactive after the failure). **Gap (3) (bespoke section-refresh blank-then-fill retrofit) deliberately left as accepted residual, not fixed a fourth time**: named precisely as `loadYardBoard`/`loadWarehouseCockpit`/`loadOMSOrders` and the same shape elsewhere in WMS/Finance — broader and more judgment-heavy than gap (2) was, and the Done bar's other five criteria are now solidly met; effort moved to BLD-041 instead per the build checklist's own priority order. `node --check`/`go build`/`go vet` clean; one full `go test ./... -p 1 -count=1` run 100% green. ## OPS — Release, Hosting & Operations

### OPS › Release pipeline & gates

- [ ] **26.11.6** Hypercare window definition (on-call owner, duration, rollback trigger) for the first pilot. **[needs user sign-off + 20.1/20.2]** — **plan drafted 2026-08-06**: `docs/operations/hypercare_plan.md`, with a stated default for each of the four decisions (14 days; named primary + secondary; business hours +2h rather than 24/7, since 24/7 for one maintainer degrades daytime response rather than adding cover; and pre-agreed rollback triggers). Reuses `incident_runbook.md`'s existing P0/P1/P2 severities and rollback mechanics rather than restating them. The rollback triggers are the substance: any P0 unfixed within 2h, two P1s in 24h, **any doubt about data integrity** (GL not balancing, stock not reconciling, an approval that appears to have bypassed maker-checker — roll back first and investigate from backup, because a corrupted ledger worsens with every transaction written on top), or two consecutive failed nightly backups. **Explicitly records its own blockers**: 20.1 (escalation contacts) and 20.2 (alert webhook) are hard preconditions — without them an out-of-hours P0 reaches nobody, which makes every response target in the plan fiction. 26.11.3's drill is also marked a precondition rather than a parallel task.

Preserve open blockers and unsupported configurations; backlog completion alone never establishes Production/Certified status.

Sixteen shard jobs, each with its own Postgres, split those two by last run's times (`release/test-times.json`, `Split-TestsByTime` in `ReleaseCommon.ps1`) and run them with `release/scripts/Invoke-TestShard.ps1`, which reruns a failure alone twice (a pass counts as flaky). When every job passes, a `promote` job fast-forwards main to that exact commit, never forcing. *Status: Building*

G10b records the build, vet and test results as `github`, and `releasegate` accepts that. G13 pushes the release commit to `candidate/<version>`, not main, and prints the run's link. `-Wait` waits for the run; `-LocalTests` runs everything here as before. The click also pulls the last run's test times and commits them. *Status: Building*
- [ ] **177.3 Deploy waits for the GitHub run.** D0f: when the last release's tests ran on GitHub and its commit is not on origin/main yet, Deploy watches that run, stops if it failed, and fetches again. `-Check` only reports it. Local commits of only the three records no longer stop a deploy. *Status: Building*

[needs decision: raise the binary limit or make it warn-only?] Recommended: raise it to 40 MiB, which still catches a sudden jump, and run the same check in G4c so the laptop and CI agree. *Status: Needs decision*

Each needs to be measured and cut, for example with prebuilt tools and work only on files that changed, until the click itself runs in about a minute. *Status: Not started*

- [ ] **56.2 SB-015 — encrypted database link.** `engines/security_baseline.go:283` blocks a
  production boot when `DATABASE_URL` names a non-loopback host with an `sslmode` that permits
  cleartext. On the droplet Postgres is on loopback so the rule sleeps; on Railway the database is
  a separate service, so it fires correctly. Add `sslmode=require` and confirm the negotiated
  connection with `SELECT ssl FROM pg_stat_ssl WHERE pid = pg_backend_pid();` — the setting being
  present is not evidence it was honoured. Prefer `verify-full` + `sslrootcert=` if Railway
  publishes a CA for its Postgres; record which mode was actually achieved and why.
- [ ] **56.3 SB-012 — proxy trust against Railway's edge.** `engines/security_baseline.go:251`
  blocks production when `TRUST_PROXY` is on and `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS` is empty, because
  `clientIP()` (`internal/server/middleware.go:606`) would then accept any caller's own
  `X-Forwarded-For` — forging the identity that login rate limiting, lockout and the audit trail
  all record. Railway does not publish its edge proxy's address range, so this is **determined
  empirically, not from the code**: with 56.1 still in warn mode, log `r.RemoteAddr` on one real
  inbound request, then pin that range (expect a private IPv6 or CGNAT block) into
  `TRUSTED_PROXY_CIDRS`. Verify by sending a request with a forged `X-Forwarded-For` and
  confirming the resolved IP is the socket peer, not the header. Record the range and the date —
  if Railway renumbers, this silently degrades to trusting a header, so it needs a re-check item
  at cutover and a note in the runbook.

Deliberately a *process wrapper* around `UAT_CHECKLIST.md` rather than a second copy of its test cases — that file says what to click, this says who runs it, in what order, what counts as a pass, and how it gets signed off, which is what this item actually asks for. Organised by **seven end-to-end business scenarios** cutting across screens (retail day, receiving, procure-to-pay, period close, separation of duties, stock accuracy, and a deliberate misuse pass) rather than screen-by-screen, because the screen-by-screen walk has been done repeatedly by AI sessions and proves screens function, not that the system supports how the business works. Includes the participant matrix (noting the approver **must** be a different person from the maker or the maker-checker scenario tests nothing), realistic-master-data prerequisites (toy data hides HSN/GST/layout problems), the defect log with a mandatory per-row decision, exit criteria, and sign-off blocks.
